Some days, I barely manage to get down a cup of coffee and maybe a granola bar before I head in for a shift. I've also noticed my scrubs have been hanging pretty loose lately, and it's not like I'm actually trying to lose any weight.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to control your weight or eating on purpose?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not trying to control it at all. It's just... I don't know, I just don't have an appetite lately. I'm definitely not doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying about my scrubs... I haven't actually stepped on a scale to get an exact number, but it definitely feels like a lot. They're just really baggy on me now. I guess when you're only getting down a little bit of food before a shift, the weight just naturally drops off.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't have any fear of gaining weight. Honestly, I wouldn't mind putting a few pounds back on so my clothes fit right again. It's really just a lack of appetite, not anything about being afraid to gain weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, depressed for sure. Most mornings I just wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. It really just feels like the day is already ruined before I even put on my badge for work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are affecting your daily life or work in any significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean, between not eating much and just feeling so down... I've started making these really avoidable mistakes at work. It's embarrassing. I have to ask my coworkers to repeat simple requests because I just can't focus. I don't trust myself to keep up with anyone or even just act normal around people right now.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of appetite the same throughout the day or are there specific times when you feel even less like eating?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being worse at any specific time. It's pretty much just a constant thing throughout the whole day. I just... I don't feel hungry at all, whether it's breakfast or dinner.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression and lack of appetite are related?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. It seems like they kind of feed into each other. When I'm feeling really down and empty, the thought of eating just doesn't even cross my mind. And then, because I'm not eating, I just feel even more drained and low. So, yeah, they definitely feel connected to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression started before your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, no, actually. The steady depression and that total loss of interest in things settled in a bit later. It's been with me for roughly the past year and four months now.\n\nDoctor: Did anything significant happen in your life around the time your appetite started to decrease?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. It was right around the time things changed at my job. The clinic I work at went through this big EMR and workflow overhaul, and it ended up changing my hours around. That was almost two years ago now, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation during this period of feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I haven't... I haven't made any kind of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I do catch myself just wishing I wouldn't wake up in the morning. Or thinking it would just be easier if I disappeared.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last two years where you felt even worse than you do now, with your depression being more severe?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's been pretty steady. I mean, it hasn't gotten worse or better, it's just... always there. Just this constant low feeling for the past year and a half.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current depression as mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I would say it's moderate. It's not so severe that I'm completely unable to function or go to work, but it's definitely more than just a mild sadness. It just makes getting through the day a lot harder than it should be.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's really just a lack of appetite, not anything about being afraid to gain weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, depressed for sure. Most mornings I just wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling. It really just feels like the day is already ruined before I even put on my badge for work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are affecting your daily life or work in any significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean, between not eating much and just feeling so down... I've started making these really avoidable mistakes at work. It's embarrassing. I have to ask my coworkers to repeat simple requests because I just can't focus. And outside of work, I've completely stopped going to my chess nights, and I haven't been out for a photography walk in weeks. I just... I don't trust myself to keep up with anyone or even just act normal around people right now.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of appetite the same throughout the day or are there specific times when you feel even less like eating?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being worse at any specific time. It's pretty much just a constant thing throughout the whole day. I just... I don't feel hungry at all, whether it's breakfast or dinner.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression and lack of appetite are related?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. It seems like they kind of feed into each other. When I'm feeling really down and empty, the thought of eating just doesn't even cross my mind. And then, because I'm not eating, I just feel even more drained and low. So, yeah, they definitely feel connected to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression started before your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, no, actually. The steady depression and that total loss of interest in things settled in a bit later. It's been with me for roughly the past year and four months now.\n\nDoctor: Did anything significant happen in your life around the time your appetite started to decrease?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. It was right around the time things changed at my job. The clinic I work at went through this big EMR and workflow overhaul, and it ended up changing my hours around. That was almost two years ago now, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation during this period of feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I haven't... I haven't made any kind of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I do catch myself just wishing I wouldn't wake up in the morning. Or thinking it would just be easier if I disappeared.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last two years where you felt even worse than you do now, with your depression being more severe?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's been pretty steady. I mean, it hasn't gotten worse or better, it's just... always there. Just this constant low feeling for the past year and a half.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current depression as mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I would say it's moderate. It's not so severe that I'm completely unable to function or go to work, but it's definitely more than just a mild sadness. It just makes getting through the day a lot harder than it should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your depression worsens or improves at specific times of the year?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any kind of pattern like that. It doesn't seem to matter if it's summer or winter, it just feels the same all year round.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any anxiety symptoms, such as feeling nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I don't really feel nervous or... on edge, like you said. It's mostly just that heavy, low feeling I mentioned. I don't really get anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange thoughts or heard any voices that others couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually have. Sometimes at work, I'll hear my name, or... just short comments. Like, I'll be out in the hallway or near the phlebotomy room, and I'll hear someone say he messed up again. But when I look around, nobody is there. And it's strange because it can happen even on days when I'm not feeling especially sad. It just happens anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do notice that. Like, a coworker might ask me a simple intake question, and I'll start explaining the EMR screen to them. But then I'll jump to talking about our staffing numbers, and then maybe to something I read for my licensure. I just kind of keep going, and I can see from their faces that I completely lost the point of what they originally asked. It's like I just can't keep my thoughts straight for them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven't made any kind of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I do catch myself just wishing I wouldn't wake up in the morning. It's been pretty steady. I mean, it hasn't gotten worse or better, it's just... always there. Just this constant low feeling for the past year and a half.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current depression as mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I would say it's moderate. It's not so severe that I'm completely unable to function or go to work, but it's definitely more than just a mild sadness. It just makes getting through the day a lot harder than it should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your depression worsens or improves at specific times of the year?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any kind of pattern like that. It doesn't seem to matter if it's summer or winter, it just feels the same all year round.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any anxiety symptoms, such as feeling nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I don't really feel nervous or... on edge, like you said. It's mostly just that heavy, low feeling I mentioned. I don't really get anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange thoughts or heard any voices that others couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually have. Sometimes at work, I'll hear my name, or... just short comments. Like, I'll be out in the hallway or near the phlebotomy room, and I'll hear someone say he messed up again. But when I look around, nobody is there. And it's strange because it can happen even on days when I'm not feeling especially sad. It just happens anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do notice that. Like, a coworker might ask me a simple intake question, and I'll start explaining the EMR screen to them. I just kind of keep going, and I can see from their faces that I completely lost the point of what they originally asked. It's like I just can't keep my thoughts straight for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble completing tasks or following through with plans due to feeling disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel disorganized. I mean, I have trouble getting things done sometimes, but that's just because I feel so drained and low, not because I can't figure out how to do it or follow a plan. The plan is fine, I just... don't have the energy for it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't trust myself to keep up with anyone or even just act normal around people right now.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of appetite the same throughout the day or are there specific times when you feel even less like eating?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being worse at any specific time. It's pretty much just a constant thing throughout the whole day. I just... I don't feel hungry at all, whether it's breakfast or dinner.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression and lack of appetite are related?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. It seems like they kind of feed into each other. When I'm feeling really down and empty, the thought of eating just doesn't even cross my mind. And then, because I'm not eating, I just feel even more drained and low. So, yeah, they definitely feel connected to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression started before your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, no, actually. The steady depression and that total loss of interest in things settled in a bit later. It's been with me for roughly the past year and four months now.\n\nDoctor: Did anything significant happen in your life around the time your appetite started to decrease?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. It was right around the time things changed at my job. The clinic I work at went through this big EMR and workflow overhaul, and it ended up changing my hours around. That was almost two years ago now, and... yeah, that's pretty much when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation during this period of feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: I haven't... I haven't made any kind of plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I do catch myself just wishing I wouldn't wake up in the morning. Or thinking it would just be easier if I disappeared.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last two years where you felt even worse than you do now, with your depression being more severe?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's been pretty steady. I mean, it hasn't gotten worse or better, it's just... always there. Just this constant low feeling for the past year and a half.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current depression as mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I would say it's moderate. It's not so severe that I'm completely unable to function or go to work, but it's definitely more than just a mild sadness. It just makes getting through the day a lot harder than it should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your depression worsens or improves at specific times of the year?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any kind of pattern like that. It doesn't seem to matter if it's summer or winter, it just feels the same all year round.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any anxiety symptoms, such as feeling nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. I don't really feel nervous or... on edge, like you said. It's mostly just that heavy, low feeling I mentioned. I don't really get anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange thoughts or heard any voices that others couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually have. Sometimes at work, I'll hear my name, or... just short comments. Like, I'll be out in the hallway or near the phlebotomy room, and I'll hear someone say he messed up again. But when I look around, nobody is there. And it's strange because it can happen even on days when I'm not feeling especially sad. It just happens anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do notice that. Like, a coworker might ask me a simple intake question, and I'll start explaining the EMR screen to them. But then I'll jump to talking about our staffing numbers, and then maybe to something I read for my licensure. I just kind of keep going, and I can see from their faces that I completely lost the point of what they originally asked. It's like I just can't keep my thoughts straight for them.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble completing tasks or following through with plans due to feeling disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel disorganized. I mean, I have trouble getting things done sometimes, but that's just because I feel so drained and low, not because I can't figure out how to do it or follow a plan. The plan is fine, I just... don't have the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or beliefs besides hearing voices?\n\nPatient: Well, I do have this... I guess it's a belief. I keep getting stuck on the idea that certain people at the clinic are watching my charting times. Like, I feel like they're leaving little signs in the schedule to show they're building a case against me or trying to get me fired. My supervisor tells me it's just the new workflow and nothing is going on, but... I just can't shake the feeling that they're doing it on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that people at the clinic are watching your charting times and trying to get you fired?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I know what my supervisor said about it just being the new workflow, but... I still can't help but feel like they're watching my charting times. It really feels like they're leaving those little signs in the schedule on purpose, like they're trying to build a case to get rid of me. I just can't let it go."
